        Idea:
# Multi-gateway Discord refactor — brief

**Profile:** `thoughtful//medium +feedback` (tier 3, standard robustness, medium depth, with feedback scaffolding)
**Branch:** `discord-multi-gateway` (cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`)
**Sprint shape:** 1–2 focused days; cross-cutting plumbing in known files

---

## 1. Goal

Support running **multiple Discord bot identities in a single FastAPI process**, one per logical bot, so that:

- **Mediator** continues to listen as its existing Discord identity (current `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`)
- **Tante Rosi** listens as a separate Discord identity (Discord application ID `1401572203954311229`)
- Future logical bots can register their own Discord identity by adding (a) a `channels` row, (b) an env var with their token, and (c) a process restart — no further code changes

The work is **wiring, not contract definition**. The routing schema (`channels(transport, address, bot_id)`, `bot_bindings`, `routing.resolve_bot`) is already multi-bot-aware. The runtime is not.

## 2. Non-goals

- Refactoring the WhatsApp transport (still single-provider; out of scope)
- Changing the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` schema (already correct)
- Changing how scopes resolve (`_resolve_scope` already keys on the bot's own address)
- Removing the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (kept as fallback during cutover — see §6)
- Onboarding Tante Rosi end-to-end (this brief covers infra only; the prod `bots` + `channels` + `bot_bindings` row inserts happen in a follow-up)
- Inbound WhatsApp routing (mediator's primary prod path may still be WhatsApp; that path is unchanged)

## 3. Current state (what's there today)

### 3.1 One token, one gateway

`app/main.py:185-190` constructs exactly one `DiscordGatewayBot` when `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`:

```python
if settings.messaging_provider.strip().lower() == "discord":
    await discord.catch_up_recent_messages(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    discord_bot = discord.DiscordGatewayBot(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    app.state.discord_bot = discord_bot
    discord_task = asyncio.create_task(discord_bot.run_forever())
```

That gateway authenticates with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` via the module-level `_token()` helper at `app/services/discord.py:50`. All REST helpers (`send_text`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`, `_rest_client`) read the same token from settings.

### 3.2 Module-level singletons

`app/services/discord.py` keeps `_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None` as a module global, initialised once in `init_client()`. A single client, a single token, a single auth header.

### 3.3 Scope resolution already keys on bot's own address

In `_handle_message` and `_handle_reaction_add` (`app/services/discord.py:478-481`):

```python
bot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()
scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None
```

`routing.resolve_bot('discord', bot_user_id)` looks up the matching `channels` row → returns `bot_id`. This already supports multiple bots; the missing piece is having multiple gateways open at once so DMs to different identities reach the process at all.

### 3.4 Pacer is a singleton too

`DiscordPacer` is constructed once in `app/main.py:120` and stored at `app.state.discord_pacer`. It holds in-memory typing state keyed by `user_id`. Its constructor takes `send_typing=discord.send_typing` — a module-level callable bound to the one token.

### 3.5 Call sites of `discord.*` helpers

Outbound helpers are called from:

- `app/services/messaging.py:260, 417` — `discord.send_text` (the main outbound path)
- `app/services/agentic.py:422` — `discord.add_reaction`
- `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1912, 1967, 2011` — `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `add_reaction`
- `app/main.py:69, 87, 115, 123` — pacer/coalescer glue calling `get_dm_channel_id`, `add_reaction`

None of these call sites currently knows or threads `bot_id`. They route by `user.phone` (which is the Discord user ID of the recipient).

## 4. Target state

### 4.1 Env var convention

```
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID_UPPER>   # one per registered Discord bot
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN                   # legacy fallback — see §6
```

Examples:
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI`

The `<BOT_ID>` segment is the same `bots.id` value (`mediator`, `tante_rosi`) uppercased and with non-alphanumeric replaced by `_`. The convention is mechanical so adding a new bot needs no code change.

### 4.2 Per-bot Discord client

Introduce a `DiscordClient` class that owns:
- One `bot_id`
- One token (`SecretStr`)
- One `httpx.AsyncClient` (its own connection pool)
- One bot user ID (decoded from token)
- All the REST methods currently in `app/services/discord.py` as **instance methods**

`DiscordGatewayBot` becomes a thin wrapper around a `DiscordClient` plus the websocket gateway loop. One `DiscordGatewayBot` per registered Discord bot.

### 4.3 Module-level helpers — facade pattern

Existing module-level helpers (`discord.send_text`, `discord.add_reaction`, …) stay, but become **facades that require a `bot_id`** (or accept `bot_id=None` and look it up from context — see §4.5). The facade routes to the correct `DiscordClient` instance from a registry.

Pseudocode:
```python
# app/services/discord.py
_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}

def get_client(bot_id: str) -> DiscordClient:
    return _clients[bot_id]

async def send_text(to: str, body: str, *, bot_id: str, send_typing_indicator: bool = True) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(to, body, send_typing_indicator=send_typing_indicator)
```

### 4.4 Per-bot client registry, populated at startup

In `lifespan`:

1. Query `channels WHERE transport='discord'` → list of `(bot_id, address)` pairs
2. For each: read env var `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` (or fall back to `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` if exactly one bot row matches — see §6)
3. Instantiate `DiscordClient(bot_id, token)` and register in `_clients[bot_id]`
4. Instantiate `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id, client, pool, coalescer)` and start the gateway task
5. Append the task to `app.state.background_tasks`

If a `channels` row exists with no matching env var, **log a warning and skip that bot** (do not fail startup — let mediator keep running even if Rosi's token is missing).

### 4.5 Threading `bot_id` through outbound call sites

Two paths to choose from in the plan:

**Option A (recommended): thread `bot_id` through.** Every call site to `discord.send_text`/`add_reaction`/etc gains a `bot_id` parameter. The caller derives `bot_id` from context:
- `messaging.py` outbound path → `bot_id` comes from the `messages.bot_id` of the row being replied to (or from the agentic turn's resolved scope)
- `agentic.py` add_reaction → from the resolved scope
- `write_tools.py` edit/delete/react → from the tool-call's resolved scope (the bot doing the call)
- `main.py` pacer glue → from the user's resolved scope (typing-state-per-bot)

**Option B (worse): infer `bot_id` from `user.phone` via a routing lookup.** Adds DB calls per send; not great.

The plan should pick A and identify exactly where each call site sources `bot_id` from. If any call site has ambiguous context (no obvious source for `bot_id`), surface it in the plan and propose a resolution.

### 4.6 Pacer state

`DiscordPacer` keeps typing state keyed by `user_id`. With two bots, the same user could be typing to mediator AND to Rosi simultaneously, and those should not collide.

**Decision needed in plan:** either
- **Per-bot pacer instances** (`app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`) — simpler reasoning, more memory
- **Single pacer, key state by `(bot_id, user_id)`** — denser but requires touching every `mark_user_typing` / `typing_state` / `clear_user_typing` signature

Recommend per-bot pacer instances; the memory cost is trivial.

### 4.7 `discord_id.py`

`discord_bot_user_id()` becomes `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str)` and reads the per-bot token. The fallback to decode from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` becomes per-bot env-var lookup with the same decoder.

## 5. Touch points (the full list)

The 12 files/areas the refactor touches:

| # | File | What changes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `app/services/discord.py` | Introduce `DiscordClient` class. Convert module-level REST helpers to facades. `DiscordGatewayBot` becomes per-bot. Module-level `_client` global removed. |
| 2 | `app/services/discord_id.py` | `discord_bot_user_id()` takes `bot_id`. New env-var convention. |
| 3 | `app/config.py` | Per-bot token discovery. Settings exposes `discord_bot_tokens: dict[str, SecretStr]` populated from env (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` pattern). Keep `discord_bot_token` for back-compat fallback. |
| 4 | `app/main.py` | Lifespan iterates `channels WHERE transport='discord'`, creates per-bot clients and gateways. Pacer becomes a dict keyed by `bot_id`. Pacer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send`) thread `bot_id`. |
| 5 | `app/services/messaging.py` | `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` thread `bot_id` to `discord.send_text`. Source: the row's `bot_id` or the agentic turn's scope. |
| 6 | `app/services/agentic.py` | `discord.add_reaction` call gains `bot_id`. Source: agentic turn's resolved scope. |
| 7 | `app/services/tools/write_tools.py` | Three call sites (edit/delete/react) gain `bot_id`. Source: each tool's resolved scope from `messages.bot_id`. |
| 8 | `app/services/pacer.py` | If we go per-bot pacer instances (recommended): no signature change. If single-pacer-with-(bot_id, user_id) keys: every method that touches typing state gains `bot_id`. |
| 9 | `scripts/seed_channels.py` | Currently seeds one Discord channel from one token. New behaviour: iterate over a configurable set of (bot_id, token) pairs from env, seed a `channels` row per pair. **Idempotent** — re-running with the same env should be a no-op. |
| 10 | `app/main.py` (startup diagnostics) | `_log_startup_diagnostics` now logs *which* per-bot tokens are configured. Don't log token values. |
| 11 | `app/services/discord.py` (`catch_up_recent_messages` and `seed_partner_users`) | Both currently use single-token REST. `seed_partner_users` is mediator-specific (seeds two partners from `DISCORD_PARTNER_USER_ID_A/B`) — keep that behaviour bound to mediator's client only. `catch_up_recent_messages` becomes per-bot. |
| 12 | Tests | Anything that mocks `discord._token()` or constructs `DiscordGatewayBot()` with the old signature needs to update. Estimated: 6–15 test files. |

## 6. Cutover strategy

The Veas Railway service is **currently live** running mediator on `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=<mediator's token>` (assumed; verify in prep phase). The deploy must not regress mediator.

### 6.1 Back-compat fallback

During the transition, support **both** env var schemes:

1. New: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` (etc.)
2. Legacy: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (singular) — treated as the token for the **single** `bot_id` in the `channels` table that matches, OR ignored if multiple Discord channels exist.

Logic at startup:
- If any per-bot env var is set, use per-bot lookup.
- If only `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` is set and exactly one `channels` row has `transport='discord'`, treat it as that bot's token (logs a deprecation warning).
- If neither matches the registered channels, log a warning and skip those bots.

This means **prod cutover is a config change, not a code-and-config simultaneous change**:

1. Deploy code with multi-gateway support. Old `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still works for mediator. No regression.
2. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR=<same value as DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN>` in Railway. Mediator now works under both schemes.
3. Insert `channels` row for `tante_rosi` (with Rosi's Discord bot user ID as address).
4. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI=<Rosi's token>` in Railway.
5. Insert `bots` row + `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`.
6. Restart. Both gateways come up.
7. After verification: delete the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var. Confirm mediator still works (it should, via `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`).

The fallback can be retired in a follow-up PR once everyone's confident.

### 6.2 What `channels` row currently exists?

The prep phase **must verify**: query `mediator.channels WHERE transport='discord'` against prod and report what's there. If a mediator `channels` row already exists with the right `address` (mediator's Discord bot user ID), great. If not, we need to insert one as part of the cutover (the legacy code apparently worked without one because of the `_resolve_scope` mediator default).

### 6.3 Test environment

Local dev / CI typically runs with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` and may have no Discord token at all. The refactor must not break:
- Startup with no Discord tokens (the gateway-loop section just doesn't run)
- Startup with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` (Discord init is skipped entirely)
- Tests that mock `discord._token()` or `discord.send_text` (these need updating, but the failure modes should be loud, not silent)

## 7. Acceptance criteria

A reviewer should be able to confirm all of these from the diff + a manual test:

1. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + only legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` set + one `channels` row → works exactly as before.** Mediator gateway opens, receives messages, replies. No deprecation noise that breaks anything.
2. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` + two `channels` rows → both gateways open.** Each handles only its own DMs. Replies route to the right bot's token (verify with a DM to each).
3. **Pacer state is per-bot.** Typing to mediator does not affect Rosi's pacer state.
4. **Adding a third bot is purely config:** insert `bots` row, insert `channels` row, set env var, restart. No code change required.
5. **All existing tests pass** with adjustments only to the discord-test surface (no broad test-suite shuffling).
6. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` is unchanged.** No Discord init runs; no errors on missing Discord env vars.
7. **`seed_channels.py` is idempotent under the new scheme** — running it twice with the same env produces no diff in the table.
8. **Startup diagnostics list which bot ids are configured for Discord and which are missing tokens.** No token values in logs.
9. **No regression in pytest collection / type checking.**

## 8. Risks and watchouts

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| **Threading `bot_id` through `messaging.py` is touchier than it looks** because outbound messages can be replies to messages that have NULL `bot_id` (legacy mediator rows). | Default to `'mediator'` when the source row's `bot_id` is NULL. Make this explicit in the plan. |
| **`agentic.py` `add_reaction` call site lacks an obvious `bot_id` source** if the reaction is fire-and-forget. | The agentic turn always has a resolved scope; thread it through. |
| **Pacer signature change ripples** if we go single-pacer-with-keys instead of per-bot-pacer. | Recommend per-bot pacer; signature unchanged. |
| **`init_client` / `close_client` module-level lifecycle** is currently keyed off the one global `_client`. With per-bot clients, the dict is the new state. | The lifespan now closes all clients in the dict. Don't forget the `close_client` call in the `finally:` of `lifespan`. |
| **Cutover env-var ordering** — if `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` is set but the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still exists, what wins? | Per-bot env vars always win. Legacy only kicks in when no per-bot match exists for a channel row. Document explicitly. |
| **Tests that mock the module-level `_token()` or `_client`** will break silently if not updated. | Search for these patterns in prep phase; produce a list of test files needing updates. |
| **The decode-from-token helper in `discord_id.py`** is called by `seed_channels.py` and by inbound handlers. Both need per-bot variants. | Pass `bot_id` explicitly to the decoder; the env var pattern handles the rest. |

## 9. Deliverables

A single branch, `discord-multi-gateway`, cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. One PR. Passing test suite. The PR description should include:

- Summary of the refactor
- The new env-var convention
- Cutover steps for prod (copy-paste of §6.1)
- A note on what's NOT in the PR (the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` row inserts for Tante Rosi are a follow-up — those need the Discord bot ID and the principal user ID, which are operator data)

## 10. Out of scope (don't do these in this PR)

- Inserting prod `bots` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `channels` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`
- Onboarding the principal user to Rosi
- Changing the WhatsApp transport
- Retiring the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (follow-up after cutover)
- Refactoring the inbound path beyond what's needed to thread `bot_id` through scope resolution (which is already in place)

## 11. References

- `app/services/discord.py:50` — `_token()` singleton
- `app/services/discord.py:329-487` — `DiscordGatewayBot` class
- `app/services/discord.py:478-481` — scope resolution that already keys on bot's own address
- `app/services/routing.py:20` — `resolve_bot(transport, address)`
- `app/services/inbound.py:169-209` — `_resolve_scope`
- `app/main.py:185-190` — single-gateway construction
- `app/main.py:118-145` — `_build_coalescer` and `DiscordPacer` construction
- `migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql` — `bots`, `channels`, `bot_bindings` schema
- `scripts/seed_channels.py` — currently single-token channel seeder

## 12. What the planner should produce

A plan that:

1. Explicitly proposes the `DiscordClient` class shape (constructor, instance methods, where it lives)
2. Lists the exact signature change for each of the 12 touch points
3. Resolves the per-bot-pacer vs single-pacer question with a reason
4. Specifies how each outbound call site sources `bot_id` (the §4.5 question)
5. Sequences the cutover-back-compat logic correctly (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` fallback under what conditions)
6. Identifies the test files that need updating (prep phase output)
7. Confirms the `seed_channels.py` idempotency strategy under the new scheme
8. Has a clear "this is what 'done' looks like" against §7

The plan is allowed to overrule any specific recommendation in this brief if it has a reason — but it must surface the disagreement explicitly, not silently.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).

- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

42	        path = url.removeprefix(DISCORD_API_BASE)
43	        request = getattr(self._client, "request", None)

45	            return await request(method, path, **kwargs)
46	        method_func = getattr(self._client, method.lower())
47	        return await method_func(path, **kwargs)

52	    if token is None or not token.get_secret_value():

        Idea:
# Multi-gateway Discord refactor — brief

**Profile:** `thoughtful//medium +feedback` (tier 3, standard robustness, medium depth, with feedback scaffolding)
**Branch:** `discord-multi-gateway` (cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`)
**Sprint shape:** 1–2 focused days; cross-cutting plumbing in known files

---

## 1. Goal

Support running **multiple Discord bot identities in a single FastAPI process**, one per logical bot, so that:

- **Mediator** continues to listen as its existing Discord identity (current `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`)
- **Tante Rosi** listens as a separate Discord identity (Discord application ID `1401572203954311229`)
- Future logical bots can register their own Discord identity by adding (a) a `channels` row, (b) an env var with their token, and (c) a process restart — no further code changes

The work is **wiring, not contract definition**. The routing schema (`channels(transport, address, bot_id)`, `bot_bindings`, `routing.resolve_bot`) is already multi-bot-aware. The runtime is not.

## 2. Non-goals

- Refactoring the WhatsApp transport (still single-provider; out of scope)
- Changing the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` schema (already correct)
- Changing how scopes resolve (`_resolve_scope` already keys on the bot's own address)
- Removing the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (kept as fallback during cutover — see §6)
- Onboarding Tante Rosi end-to-end (this brief covers infra only; the prod `bots` + `channels` + `bot_bindings` row inserts happen in a follow-up)
- Inbound WhatsApp routing (mediator's primary prod path may still be WhatsApp; that path is unchanged)

## 3. Current state (what's there today)

### 3.1 One token, one gateway

`app/main.py:185-190` constructs exactly one `DiscordGatewayBot` when `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`:

```python
if settings.messaging_provider.strip().lower() == "discord":
    await discord.catch_up_recent_messages(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    discord_bot = discord.DiscordGatewayBot(pool, app.state.coalescer)
    app.state.discord_bot = discord_bot
    discord_task = asyncio.create_task(discord_bot.run_forever())
```

That gateway authenticates with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` via the module-level `_token()` helper at `app/services/discord.py:50`. All REST helpers (`send_text`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`, `_rest_client`) read the same token from settings.

### 3.2 Module-level singletons

`app/services/discord.py` keeps `_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None` as a module global, initialised once in `init_client()`. A single client, a single token, a single auth header.

### 3.3 Scope resolution already keys on bot's own address

In `_handle_message` and `_handle_reaction_add` (`app/services/discord.py:478-481`):

```python
bot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()
scope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None
```

`routing.resolve_bot('discord', bot_user_id)` looks up the matching `channels` row → returns `bot_id`. This already supports multiple bots; the missing piece is having multiple gateways open at once so DMs to different identities reach the process at all.

### 3.4 Pacer is a singleton too

`DiscordPacer` is constructed once in `app/main.py:120` and stored at `app.state.discord_pacer`. It holds in-memory typing state keyed by `user_id`. Its constructor takes `send_typing=discord.send_typing` — a module-level callable bound to the one token.

### 3.5 Call sites of `discord.*` helpers

Outbound helpers are called from:

- `app/services/messaging.py:260, 417` — `discord.send_text` (the main outbound path)
- `app/services/agentic.py:422` — `discord.add_reaction`
- `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1912, 1967, 2011` — `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `add_reaction`
- `app/main.py:69, 87, 115, 123` — pacer/coalescer glue calling `get_dm_channel_id`, `add_reaction`

None of these call sites currently knows or threads `bot_id`. They route by `user.phone` (which is the Discord user ID of the recipient).

## 4. Target state

### 4.1 Env var convention

```
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID_UPPER>   # one per registered Discord bot
DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN                   # legacy fallback — see §6
```

Examples:
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`
- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI`

The `<BOT_ID>` segment is the same `bots.id` value (`mediator`, `tante_rosi`) uppercased and with non-alphanumeric replaced by `_`. The convention is mechanical so adding a new bot needs no code change.

### 4.2 Per-bot Discord client

Introduce a `DiscordClient` class that owns:
- One `bot_id`
- One token (`SecretStr`)
- One `httpx.AsyncClient` (its own connection pool)
- One bot user ID (decoded from token)
- All the REST methods currently in `app/services/discord.py` as **instance methods**

`DiscordGatewayBot` becomes a thin wrapper around a `DiscordClient` plus the websocket gateway loop. One `DiscordGatewayBot` per registered Discord bot.

### 4.3 Module-level helpers — facade pattern

Existing module-level helpers (`discord.send_text`, `discord.add_reaction`, …) stay, but become **facades that require a `bot_id`** (or accept `bot_id=None` and look it up from context — see §4.5). The facade routes to the correct `DiscordClient` instance from a registry.

Pseudocode:
```python
# app/services/discord.py
_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}

def get_client(bot_id: str) -> DiscordClient:
    return _clients[bot_id]

async def send_text(to: str, body: str, *, bot_id: str, send_typing_indicator: bool = True) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(to, body, send_typing_indicator=send_typing_indicator)
```

### 4.4 Per-bot client registry, populated at startup

In `lifespan`:

1. Query `channels WHERE transport='discord'` → list of `(bot_id, address)` pairs
2. For each: read env var `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` (or fall back to `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` if exactly one bot row matches — see §6)
3. Instantiate `DiscordClient(bot_id, token)` and register in `_clients[bot_id]`
4. Instantiate `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id, client, pool, coalescer)` and start the gateway task
5. Append the task to `app.state.background_tasks`

If a `channels` row exists with no matching env var, **log a warning and skip that bot** (do not fail startup — let mediator keep running even if Rosi's token is missing).

### 4.5 Threading `bot_id` through outbound call sites

Two paths to choose from in the plan:

**Option A (recommended): thread `bot_id` through.** Every call site to `discord.send_text`/`add_reaction`/etc gains a `bot_id` parameter. The caller derives `bot_id` from context:
- `messaging.py` outbound path → `bot_id` comes from the `messages.bot_id` of the row being replied to (or from the agentic turn's resolved scope)
- `agentic.py` add_reaction → from the resolved scope
- `write_tools.py` edit/delete/react → from the tool-call's resolved scope (the bot doing the call)
- `main.py` pacer glue → from the user's resolved scope (typing-state-per-bot)

**Option B (worse): infer `bot_id` from `user.phone` via a routing lookup.** Adds DB calls per send; not great.

The plan should pick A and identify exactly where each call site sources `bot_id` from. If any call site has ambiguous context (no obvious source for `bot_id`), surface it in the plan and propose a resolution.

### 4.6 Pacer state

`DiscordPacer` keeps typing state keyed by `user_id`. With two bots, the same user could be typing to mediator AND to Rosi simultaneously, and those should not collide.

**Decision needed in plan:** either
- **Per-bot pacer instances** (`app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`) — simpler reasoning, more memory
- **Single pacer, key state by `(bot_id, user_id)`** — denser but requires touching every `mark_user_typing` / `typing_state` / `clear_user_typing` signature

Recommend per-bot pacer instances; the memory cost is trivial.

### 4.7 `discord_id.py`

`discord_bot_user_id()` becomes `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str)` and reads the per-bot token. The fallback to decode from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` becomes per-bot env-var lookup with the same decoder.

## 5. Touch points (the full list)

The 12 files/areas the refactor touches:

| # | File | What changes |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `app/services/discord.py` | Introduce `DiscordClient` class. Convert module-level REST helpers to facades. `DiscordGatewayBot` becomes per-bot. Module-level `_client` global removed. |
| 2 | `app/services/discord_id.py` | `discord_bot_user_id()` takes `bot_id`. New env-var convention. |
| 3 | `app/config.py` | Per-bot token discovery. Settings exposes `discord_bot_tokens: dict[str, SecretStr]` populated from env (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` pattern). Keep `discord_bot_token` for back-compat fallback. |
| 4 | `app/main.py` | Lifespan iterates `channels WHERE transport='discord'`, creates per-bot clients and gateways. Pacer becomes a dict keyed by `bot_id`. Pacer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send`) thread `bot_id`. |
| 5 | `app/services/messaging.py` | `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` thread `bot_id` to `discord.send_text`. Source: the row's `bot_id` or the agentic turn's scope. |
| 6 | `app/services/agentic.py` | `discord.add_reaction` call gains `bot_id`. Source: agentic turn's resolved scope. |
| 7 | `app/services/tools/write_tools.py` | Three call sites (edit/delete/react) gain `bot_id`. Source: each tool's resolved scope from `messages.bot_id`. |
| 8 | `app/services/pacer.py` | If we go per-bot pacer instances (recommended): no signature change. If single-pacer-with-(bot_id, user_id) keys: every method that touches typing state gains `bot_id`. |
| 9 | `scripts/seed_channels.py` | Currently seeds one Discord channel from one token. New behaviour: iterate over a configurable set of (bot_id, token) pairs from env, seed a `channels` row per pair. **Idempotent** — re-running with the same env should be a no-op. |
| 10 | `app/main.py` (startup diagnostics) | `_log_startup_diagnostics` now logs *which* per-bot tokens are configured. Don't log token values. |
| 11 | `app/services/discord.py` (`catch_up_recent_messages` and `seed_partner_users`) | Both currently use single-token REST. `seed_partner_users` is mediator-specific (seeds two partners from `DISCORD_PARTNER_USER_ID_A/B`) — keep that behaviour bound to mediator's client only. `catch_up_recent_messages` becomes per-bot. |
| 12 | Tests | Anything that mocks `discord._token()` or constructs `DiscordGatewayBot()` with the old signature needs to update. Estimated: 6–15 test files. |

## 6. Cutover strategy

The Veas Railway service is **currently live** running mediator on `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=<mediator's token>` (assumed; verify in prep phase). The deploy must not regress mediator.

### 6.1 Back-compat fallback

During the transition, support **both** env var schemes:

1. New: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` (etc.)
2. Legacy: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (singular) — treated as the token for the **single** `bot_id` in the `channels` table that matches, OR ignored if multiple Discord channels exist.

Logic at startup:
- If any per-bot env var is set, use per-bot lookup.
- If only `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` is set and exactly one `channels` row has `transport='discord'`, treat it as that bot's token (logs a deprecation warning).
- If neither matches the registered channels, log a warning and skip those bots.

This means **prod cutover is a config change, not a code-and-config simultaneous change**:

1. Deploy code with multi-gateway support. Old `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still works for mediator. No regression.
2. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR=<same value as DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN>` in Railway. Mediator now works under both schemes.
3. Insert `channels` row for `tante_rosi` (with Rosi's Discord bot user ID as address).
4. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI=<Rosi's token>` in Railway.
5. Insert `bots` row + `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`.
6. Restart. Both gateways come up.
7. After verification: delete the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var. Confirm mediator still works (it should, via `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`).

The fallback can be retired in a follow-up PR once everyone's confident.

### 6.2 What `channels` row currently exists?

The prep phase **must verify**: query `mediator.channels WHERE transport='discord'` against prod and report what's there. If a mediator `channels` row already exists with the right `address` (mediator's Discord bot user ID), great. If not, we need to insert one as part of the cutover (the legacy code apparently worked without one because of the `_resolve_scope` mediator default).

### 6.3 Test environment

Local dev / CI typically runs with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` and may have no Discord token at all. The refactor must not break:
- Startup with no Discord tokens (the gateway-loop section just doesn't run)
- Startup with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` (Discord init is skipped entirely)
- Tests that mock `discord._token()` or `discord.send_text` (these need updating, but the failure modes should be loud, not silent)

## 7. Acceptance criteria

A reviewer should be able to confirm all of these from the diff + a manual test:

1. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + only legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` set + one `channels` row → works exactly as before.** Mediator gateway opens, receives messages, replies. No deprecation noise that breaks anything.
2. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` + two `channels` rows → both gateways open.** Each handles only its own DMs. Replies route to the right bot's token (verify with a DM to each).
3. **Pacer state is per-bot.** Typing to mediator does not affect Rosi's pacer state.
4. **Adding a third bot is purely config:** insert `bots` row, insert `channels` row, set env var, restart. No code change required.
5. **All existing tests pass** with adjustments only to the discord-test surface (no broad test-suite shuffling).
6. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` is unchanged.** No Discord init runs; no errors on missing Discord env vars.
7. **`seed_channels.py` is idempotent under the new scheme** — running it twice with the same env produces no diff in the table.
8. **Startup diagnostics list which bot ids are configured for Discord and which are missing tokens.** No token values in logs.
9. **No regression in pytest collection / type checking.**

## 8. Risks and watchouts

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| **Threading `bot_id` through `messaging.py` is touchier than it looks** because outbound messages can be replies to messages that have NULL `bot_id` (legacy mediator rows). | Default to `'mediator'` when the source row's `bot_id` is NULL. Make this explicit in the plan. |
| **`agentic.py` `add_reaction` call site lacks an obvious `bot_id` source** if the reaction is fire-and-forget. | The agentic turn always has a resolved scope; thread it through. |
| **Pacer signature change ripples** if we go single-pacer-with-keys instead of per-bot-pacer. | Recommend per-bot pacer; signature unchanged. |
| **`init_client` / `close_client` module-level lifecycle** is currently keyed off the one global `_client`. With per-bot clients, the dict is the new state. | The lifespan now closes all clients in the dict. Don't forget the `close_client` call in the `finally:` of `lifespan`. |
| **Cutover env-var ordering** — if `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` is set but the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still exists, what wins? | Per-bot env vars always win. Legacy only kicks in when no per-bot match exists for a channel row. Document explicitly. |
| **Tests that mock the module-level `_token()` or `_client`** will break silently if not updated. | Search for these patterns in prep phase; produce a list of test files needing updates. |
| **The decode-from-token helper in `discord_id.py`** is called by `seed_channels.py` and by inbound handlers. Both need per-bot variants. | Pass `bot_id` explicitly to the decoder; the env var pattern handles the rest. |

## 9. Deliverables

A single branch, `discord-multi-gateway`, cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. One PR. Passing test suite. The PR description should include:

- Summary of the refactor
- The new env-var convention
- Cutover steps for prod (copy-paste of §6.1)
- A note on what's NOT in the PR (the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` row inserts for Tante Rosi are a follow-up — those need the Discord bot ID and the principal user ID, which are operator data)

## 10. Out of scope (don't do these in this PR)

- Inserting prod `bots` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `channels` row for `tante_rosi`
- Inserting prod `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`
- Onboarding the principal user to Rosi
- Changing the WhatsApp transport
- Retiring the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (follow-up after cutover)
- Refactoring the inbound path beyond what's needed to thread `bot_id` through scope resolution (which is already in place)

## 11. References

- `app/services/discord.py:50` — `_token()` singleton
- `app/services/discord.py:329-487` — `DiscordGatewayBot` class
- `app/services/discord.py:478-481` — scope resolution that already keys on bot's own address
- `app/services/routing.py:20` — `resolve_bot(transport, address)`
- `app/services/inbound.py:169-209` — `_resolve_scope`
- `app/main.py:185-190` — single-gateway construction
- `app/main.py:118-145` — `_build_coalescer` and `DiscordPacer` construction
- `migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql` — `bots`, `channels`, `bot_bindings` schema
- `scripts/seed_channels.py` — currently single-token channel seeder

## 12. What the planner should produce

A plan that:

1. Explicitly proposes the `DiscordClient` class shape (constructor, instance methods, where it lives)
2. Lists the exact signature change for each of the 12 touch points
3. Resolves the per-bot-pacer vs single-pacer question with a reason
4. Specifies how each outbound call site sources `bot_id` (the §4.5 question)
5. Sequences the cutover-back-compat logic correctly (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` fallback under what conditions)
6. Identifies the test files that need updating (prep phase output)
7. Confirms the `seed_channels.py` idempotency strategy under the new scheme
8. Has a clear "this is what 'done' looks like" against §7

The plan is allowed to overrule any specific recommendation in this brief if it has a reason — but it must surface the disagreement explicitly, not silently.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Multi-gateway Discord Refactor

## Overview
Today the FastAPI process opens exactly one Discord gateway, authenticated with a single module-level token (`app/services/discord.py:50`), constructed once in `lifespan` (`app/main.py:185-190`). The routing schema already supports multiple bots (`channels.bot_id`, `routing.resolve_bot`, `_resolve_scope`), so the missing piece is purely runtime plumbing: per-bot HTTP clients, per-bot gateway loops, and threading `bot_id` through every outbound helper.

Goal: support N Discord identities in one process, registered by adding a `channels` row + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` env var, with a legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` fallback so the live mediator deploy can cut over without a code+config flip in the same step. Stay aligned with §4–§6 of the brief; deviations (if any) are surfaced below in assumptions.

Branch: `discord-multi-gateway`, cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. Out-of-scope: prod `bots`/`channels`/`bot_bindings` inserts for Tante Rosi, WhatsApp transport changes, removal of legacy env var.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Config, Client class, ID helper

### Step 1: Audit current Discord runtime (`app/services/discord.py`, `app/services/discord_id.py`, `app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirm** the module-level singleton surface to convert: `_client` global (`app/services/discord.py:34`), `_token()` (`:50`), `_headers()` (`:57`), `_rest_client()` (`:61`), `init_client()` / `close_client()` (`:85-96`), the public REST helpers (`send_text`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`), `DiscordGatewayBot` (`:329-487`), `catch_up_recent_messages`, `seed_partner_users`.
2. **Confirm** scope resolution already keys on bot address (`app/services/discord.py:478-481`) — only the gateway count needs to change, not the routing logic.
3. **Note** all module-level helper callers to thread `bot_id`: `app/services/messaging.py:260,417`; `app/services/agentic.py:422`; `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1912,1967,2011`; `app/main.py:69,87,115,123`.

### Step 2: Per-bot token discovery in settings (`app/config.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a settings accessor `discord_bot_tokens() -> dict[str, SecretStr]` that scans environment for `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<UPPER_BOT_ID>` (regex `^DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_([A-Z0-9_]+)$`, lowercased back to bot_id). Keep `discord_bot_token` field for legacy fallback. Do not load tokens into a frozen pydantic field — read env at lifespan time so missing-token errors are localized to startup logging, not import.
2. **Document** the env-var convention in a one-line comment next to the field.

### Step 3: Introduce `DiscordClient` class (`app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Create** `class DiscordClient` owning: `bot_id: str`, `_token: str`, `_http: httpx.AsyncClient`, `bot_user_id: str` (decoded from token at construct time). Lifecycle: `__init__(bot_id, token)`, `async def aclose()`.
2. **Move** the REST helpers in as instance methods: `send_text`, `send_template`, `send_typing`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `get_dm_channel_id`, `_rest_client`, `_headers`. Each uses `self._http` and `self._token`.
3. **Add** a module-level registry: `_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}` plus `register_client(client)`, `get_client(bot_id) -> DiscordClient`, `iter_clients()`, `async def close_all_clients()`.
4. **Replace** module-level helpers with thin facades that take `*, bot_id: str` and delegate: `async def send_text(to, body, *, bot_id, send_typing_indicator=True) -> dict: return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(...)`. Same pattern for `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`. Remove `_client`/`init_client`/`close_client`/`_token`/`_get_client` module globals.
5. **Update** `_send_typing_after_delay` to take `bot_id` and call the facade.

### Step 4: Per-bot bot-user-id (`app/services/discord_id.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Change** `discord_bot_user_id()` → `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str) -> str | None`. Token source: `get_settings().discord_bot_tokens().get(bot_id)`, falling back to legacy `discord_bot_token` only when called as `discord_bot_user_id('mediator')` AND exactly one Discord channel row exists (encapsulate this in a `resolve_discord_token(bot_id, channels_rows) -> str | None` helper that lives in `discord_id.py` or `discord.py`).
2. **Keep** the JWT-segment decoder unchanged — only the token source changes.

## Phase 2: Gateway + Lifespan wiring

### Step 5: Per-bot `DiscordGatewayBot` (`app/services/discord.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Change** constructor to `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id: str, client: DiscordClient, pool, coalescer)`. Store `self.bot_id`, `self.client`.
2. **Replace** every internal use of `discord_bot_user_id()` with `self.client.bot_user_id`. Replace every call into module-level helpers with `self.client.<method>(...)` (or the facade with `bot_id=self.bot_id`).
3. **Update** `_handle_message` and `_handle_reaction_add` to pass `self.bot_id` to scope resolution context where useful (the existing `routing.resolve_bot('discord', bot_user_id)` call is already correct since `bot_user_id` now comes from `self.client.bot_user_id`).
4. **Convert** `catch_up_recent_messages(pool, coalescer)` to `catch_up_recent_messages(pool, coalescer, *, client: DiscordClient)` — uses the bot's own REST client.
5. **Scope** `seed_partner_users` (mediator-specific) to be called only with the mediator client; leave it gated by an explicit `if bot_id == 'mediator'` in lifespan.

### Step 6: Per-bot `DiscordPacer` instances (`app/main.py`, `app/services/pacer.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Decision (per brief §4.6 — recommended path):** instantiate one `DiscordPacer` per bot. No signature change to `DiscordPacer` itself.
2. **Replace** `app.state.discord_pacer` with `app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`.
3. **Update** the pacer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send` in `_build_coalescer`) to be constructed per-bot, capturing `bot_id`, so calls into `discord.add_reaction`/`get_dm_channel_id` pass that `bot_id`.

### Step 7: Lifespan registration loop (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Query** `SELECT bot_id, address FROM channels WHERE transport='discord'` once at startup.
2. **For each row:** resolve token via `resolve_discord_token(bot_id, rows)`. If absent, log a warning and skip. If present, construct `DiscordClient(bot_id, token)`, register, then build the per-bot `DiscordPacer`, the coalescer glue closures, the `DiscordGatewayBot`, and launch `bot.run_forever()` as a background task. Append to `app.state.background_tasks`.
3. **If no channels rows exist** but legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` is set and `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`, synthesize a single mediator entry (back-compat for pre-channels-row prod). Log a deprecation warning.
4. **Teardown:** in `finally:`, call `await discord.close_all_clients()`.
5. **Startup diagnostics** (`_log_startup_diagnostics`): list configured bot_ids and which have tokens. Never log token values.

### Step 8: Update `seed_channels.py` (`scripts/seed_channels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Iterate** over `get_settings().discord_bot_tokens()` (plus legacy fallback for the single `mediator` case). For each `(bot_id, token)`, decode the bot user id and `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (transport, address) DO NOTHING` (or `DO UPDATE SET bot_id=EXCLUDED.bot_id` if `bot_id` may have drifted). Idempotent.
2. **Skip silently** bots whose token cannot be decoded, with a warning log.

## Phase 3: Thread `bot_id` through outbound callers

### Step 9: Messaging outbound (`app/services/messaging.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Identify** the two call sites (`:260`, `:417`). The outbound row being sent has a `bot_id` column — thread that into `discord.send_text(..., bot_id=row['bot_id'] or 'mediator')`.
2. **Default** to `'mediator'` when the source row's `bot_id` is NULL (legacy mediator rows pre-multi-bot). Make this default an explicit `_DEFAULT_BOT_ID = 'mediator'` module constant with a comment naming the back-compat reason.
3. **Update** any helper that constructs the outbound row to populate `bot_id` from the scope when available.

### Step 10: Agentic + write tools (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`agentic.py:422`** (add_reaction): source `bot_id` from the resolved scope of the current agentic turn (the turn already carries it; if not, add it to the turn context).
2. **`write_tools.py:1912,1967,2011`**: each tool call resolves a `bot_id` from `messages.bot_id` of the message being acted on (or from the tool-invocation scope). Thread it into the facade calls.

### Step 11: `main.py` pacer-glue call sites (`app/main.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Lines `69, 87, 115, 123`** invoke `discord.get_dm_channel_id` and `discord.add_reaction` inside the coalescer glue. Since glue is now constructed per-bot (Step 6), the surrounding closure already knows `bot_id` — pass it.

## Phase 4: Tests and verification

### Step 12: Test updates (`tests/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Identify** test files that patch `discord._token`, `discord._client`, `discord.send_text` at the module level, or construct `DiscordGatewayBot(pool, coalescer)` with the old signature. Run a grep over `tests/` for `discord._token`, `discord._client`, `DiscordGatewayBot(`, `discord.send_text`, `discord.add_reaction`, `discord_bot_user_id(`.
2. **Update** them to: register a stub `DiscordClient` in `_clients` under a known `bot_id` (e.g. `'mediator'`); pass `bot_id='mediator'` to facade calls; update `DiscordGatewayBot(...)` constructions to the new signature.
3. **Add** a thin new test: `test_discord_multi_gateway.py` covering (a) two registered bots → two clients in the registry, (b) facade dispatches to the right client by `bot_id`, (c) legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`-only env starts a single mediator gateway, (d) missing token for a registered channel row logs a warning and skips without raising.

### Step 13: Validation runs
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the focused new tests first: `pytest tests/test_discord_multi_gateway.py -x` (after writing them in Step 12).
2. **Run** the rest of the Discord-touching test surface: `pytest tests/ -k discord -x`.
3. **Run** the full suite: `pytest`.
4. **Type/lint** whatever the repo runs (`ruff`, `mypy` — invoke whatever `make check` / pre-commit currently uses).

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (config + DiscordClient + discord_id) — no behavior change yet; module-level facades preserve call-site signatures except for the new mandatory `bot_id` kwarg.
2. Phase 2 (gateway + lifespan + pacer + seed script) — wires up multi-gateway. After this, the app boots with N gateways but callers may still pass implicit defaults.
3. Phase 3 (thread `bot_id`) — fixes the call sites; mediator-default fallback covers legacy NULL rows.
4. Phase 4 (tests + verification) — proves it.

## Validation Order
1. New multi-gateway unit tests.
2. Discord-tagged tests across the suite.
3. Full pytest.
4. Manual smoke check listed against acceptance §7 in the brief (info-only).

  "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "decision": "DiscordClient class owns one bot_id, one token, one httpx.AsyncClient, and all REST methods as instance methods. Module-level helpers become facades requiring bot_id.",
      "rationale": "Clean separation, no shared state between bot identities."
    },
    {
      "decision": "Per-bot DiscordPacer instances (dict keyed by bot_id) rather than single pacer with composite keys.",
      "rationale": "Simpler reasoning, no signature changes to DiscordPacer itself, trivial memory cost."
    },
    {
      "decision": "Option A for outbound bot_id threading \u2014 explicit bot_id parameter at every call site rather than inferring via routing lookup.",
      "rationale": "Avoids extra DB calls per send; callers already have context to derive bot_id."
    },
    {
      "decision": "Legacy DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN fallback: per-bot env vars always win; legacy only kicks in when no per-bot match exists for a channel row.",
      "rationale": "Per brief \u00a76.1, enables clean cutover without code+config simultaneous change."
    }

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Multi-gateway Discord refactor \u2014 brief\n\n**Profile:** `thoughtful//medium +feedback` (tier 3, standard robustness, medium depth, with feedback scaffolding)\n**Branch:** `discord-multi-gateway` (cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`)\n**Sprint shape:** 1\u20132 focused days; cross-cutting plumbing in known files\n\n---\n\n## 1. Goal\n\nSupport running **multiple Discord bot identities in a single FastAPI process**, one per logical bot, so that:\n\n- **Mediator** continues to listen as its existing Discord identity (current `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`)\n- **Tante Rosi** listens as a separate Discord identity (Discord application ID `1401572203954311229`)\n- Future logical bots can register their own Discord identity by adding (a) a `channels` row, (b) an env var with their token, and (c) a process restart \u2014 no further code changes\n\nThe work is **wiring, not contract definition**. The routing schema (`channels(transport, address, bot_id)`, `bot_bindings`, `routing.resolve_bot`) is already multi-bot-aware. The runtime is not.\n\n## 2. Non-goals\n\n- Refactoring the WhatsApp transport (still single-provider; out of scope)\n- Changing the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` schema (already correct)\n- Changing how scopes resolve (`_resolve_scope` already keys on the bot's own address)\n- Removing the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (kept as fallback during cutover \u2014 see \u00a76)\n- Onboarding Tante Rosi end-to-end (this brief covers infra only; the prod `bots` + `channels` + `bot_bindings` row inserts happen in a follow-up)\n- Inbound WhatsApp routing (mediator's primary prod path may still be WhatsApp; that path is unchanged)\n\n## 3. Current state (what's there today)\n\n### 3.1 One token, one gateway\n\n`app/main.py:185-190` constructs exactly one `DiscordGatewayBot` when `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord`:\n\n```python\nif settings.messaging_provider.strip().lower() == \"discord\":\n    await discord.catch_up_recent_messages(pool, app.state.coalescer)\n    discord_bot = discord.DiscordGatewayBot(pool, app.state.coalescer)\n    app.state.discord_bot = discord_bot\n    discord_task = asyncio.create_task(discord_bot.run_forever())\n```\n\nThat gateway authenticates with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` via the module-level `_token()` helper at `app/services/discord.py:50`. All REST helpers (`send_text`, `add_reaction`, `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `send_typing`, `send_template`, `get_dm_channel_id`, `_rest_client`) read the same token from settings.\n\n### 3.2 Module-level singletons\n\n`app/services/discord.py` keeps `_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None` as a module global, initialised once in `init_client()`. A single client, a single token, a single auth header.\n\n### 3.3 Scope resolution already keys on bot's own address\n\nIn `_handle_message` and `_handle_reaction_add` (`app/services/discord.py:478-481`):\n\n```python\nbot_user_id = discord_bot_user_id()\nscope = await _resolve_scope(self.pool, 'discord', bot_user_id) if bot_user_id else None\n```\n\n`routing.resolve_bot('discord', bot_user_id)` looks up the matching `channels` row \u2192 returns `bot_id`. This already supports multiple bots; the missing piece is having multiple gateways open at once so DMs to different identities reach the process at all.\n\n### 3.4 Pacer is a singleton too\n\n`DiscordPacer` is constructed once in `app/main.py:120` and stored at `app.state.discord_pacer`. It holds in-memory typing state keyed by `user_id`. Its constructor takes `send_typing=discord.send_typing` \u2014 a module-level callable bound to the one token.\n\n### 3.5 Call sites of `discord.*` helpers\n\nOutbound helpers are called from:\n\n- `app/services/messaging.py:260, 417` \u2014 `discord.send_text` (the main outbound path)\n- `app/services/agentic.py:422` \u2014 `discord.add_reaction`\n- `app/services/tools/write_tools.py:1912, 1967, 2011` \u2014 `edit_text`, `delete_text`, `add_reaction`\n- `app/main.py:69, 87, 115, 123` \u2014 pacer/coalescer glue calling `get_dm_channel_id`, `add_reaction`\n\nNone of these call sites currently knows or threads `bot_id`. They route by `user.phone` (which is the Discord user ID of the recipient).\n\n## 4. Target state\n\n### 4.1 Env var convention\n\n```\nDISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID_UPPER>   # one per registered Discord bot\nDISCORD_BOT_TOKEN                   # legacy fallback \u2014 see \u00a76\n```\n\nExamples:\n- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`\n- `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI`\n\nThe `<BOT_ID>` segment is the same `bots.id` value (`mediator`, `tante_rosi`) uppercased and with non-alphanumeric replaced by `_`. The convention is mechanical so adding a new bot needs no code change.\n\n### 4.2 Per-bot Discord client\n\nIntroduce a `DiscordClient` class that owns:\n- One `bot_id`\n- One token (`SecretStr`)\n- One `httpx.AsyncClient` (its own connection pool)\n- One bot user ID (decoded from token)\n- All the REST methods currently in `app/services/discord.py` as **instance methods**\n\n`DiscordGatewayBot` becomes a thin wrapper around a `DiscordClient` plus the websocket gateway loop. One `DiscordGatewayBot` per registered Discord bot.\n\n### 4.3 Module-level helpers \u2014 facade pattern\n\nExisting module-level helpers (`discord.send_text`, `discord.add_reaction`, \u2026) stay, but become **facades that require a `bot_id`** (or accept `bot_id=None` and look it up from context \u2014 see \u00a74.5). The facade routes to the correct `DiscordClient` instance from a registry.\n\nPseudocode:\n```python\n# app/services/discord.py\n_clients: dict[str, DiscordClient] = {}\n\ndef get_client(bot_id: str) -> DiscordClient:\n    return _clients[bot_id]\n\nasync def send_text(to: str, body: str, *, bot_id: str, send_typing_indicator: bool = True) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    return await get_client(bot_id).send_text(to, body, send_typing_indicator=send_typing_indicator)\n```\n\n### 4.4 Per-bot client registry, populated at startup\n\nIn `lifespan`:\n\n1. Query `channels WHERE transport='discord'` \u2192 list of `(bot_id, address)` pairs\n2. For each: read env var `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` (or fall back to `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` if exactly one bot row matches \u2014 see \u00a76)\n3. Instantiate `DiscordClient(bot_id, token)` and register in `_clients[bot_id]`\n4. Instantiate `DiscordGatewayBot(bot_id, client, pool, coalescer)` and start the gateway task\n5. Append the task to `app.state.background_tasks`\n\nIf a `channels` row exists with no matching env var, **log a warning and skip that bot** (do not fail startup \u2014 let mediator keep running even if Rosi's token is missing).\n\n### 4.5 Threading `bot_id` through outbound call sites\n\nTwo paths to choose from in the plan:\n\n**Option A (recommended): thread `bot_id` through.** Every call site to `discord.send_text`/`add_reaction`/etc gains a `bot_id` parameter. The caller derives `bot_id` from context:\n- `messaging.py` outbound path \u2192 `bot_id` comes from the `messages.bot_id` of the row being replied to (or from the agentic turn's resolved scope)\n- `agentic.py` add_reaction \u2192 from the resolved scope\n- `write_tools.py` edit/delete/react \u2192 from the tool-call's resolved scope (the bot doing the call)\n- `main.py` pacer glue \u2192 from the user's resolved scope (typing-state-per-bot)\n\n**Option B (worse): infer `bot_id` from `user.phone` via a routing lookup.** Adds DB calls per send; not great.\n\nThe plan should pick A and identify exactly where each call site sources `bot_id` from. If any call site has ambiguous context (no obvious source for `bot_id`), surface it in the plan and propose a resolution.\n\n### 4.6 Pacer state\n\n`DiscordPacer` keeps typing state keyed by `user_id`. With two bots, the same user could be typing to mediator AND to Rosi simultaneously, and those should not collide.\n\n**Decision needed in plan:** either\n- **Per-bot pacer instances** (`app.state.discord_pacers: dict[str, DiscordPacer]`) \u2014 simpler reasoning, more memory\n- **Single pacer, key state by `(bot_id, user_id)`** \u2014 denser but requires touching every `mark_user_typing` / `typing_state` / `clear_user_typing` signature\n\nRecommend per-bot pacer instances; the memory cost is trivial.\n\n### 4.7 `discord_id.py`\n\n`discord_bot_user_id()` becomes `discord_bot_user_id(bot_id: str)` and reads the per-bot token. The fallback to decode from `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` becomes per-bot env-var lookup with the same decoder.\n\n## 5. Touch points (the full list)\n\nThe 12 files/areas the refactor touches:\n\n| # | File | What changes |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `app/services/discord.py` | Introduce `DiscordClient` class. Convert module-level REST helpers to facades. `DiscordGatewayBot` becomes per-bot. Module-level `_client` global removed. |\n| 2 | `app/services/discord_id.py` | `discord_bot_user_id()` takes `bot_id`. New env-var convention. |\n| 3 | `app/config.py` | Per-bot token discovery. Settings exposes `discord_bot_tokens: dict[str, SecretStr]` populated from env (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_<BOT_ID>` pattern). Keep `discord_bot_token` for back-compat fallback. |\n| 4 | `app/main.py` | Lifespan iterates `channels WHERE transport='discord'`, creates per-bot clients and gateways. Pacer becomes a dict keyed by `bot_id`. Pacer-glue closures (`on_live_typing`, `before_paced_send`) thread `bot_id`. |\n| 5 | `app/services/messaging.py` | `send_outbound` and `send_outbound_part` thread `bot_id` to `discord.send_text`. Source: the row's `bot_id` or the agentic turn's scope. |\n| 6 | `app/services/agentic.py` | `discord.add_reaction` call gains `bot_id`. Source: agentic turn's resolved scope. |\n| 7 | `app/services/tools/write_tools.py` | Three call sites (edit/delete/react) gain `bot_id`. Source: each tool's resolved scope from `messages.bot_id`. |\n| 8 | `app/services/pacer.py` | If we go per-bot pacer instances (recommended): no signature change. If single-pacer-with-(bot_id, user_id) keys: every method that touches typing state gains `bot_id`. |\n| 9 | `scripts/seed_channels.py` | Currently seeds one Discord channel from one token. New behaviour: iterate over a configurable set of (bot_id, token) pairs from env, seed a `channels` row per pair. **Idempotent** \u2014 re-running with the same env should be a no-op. |\n| 10 | `app/main.py` (startup diagnostics) | `_log_startup_diagnostics` now logs *which* per-bot tokens are configured. Don't log token values. |\n| 11 | `app/services/discord.py` (`catch_up_recent_messages` and `seed_partner_users`) | Both currently use single-token REST. `seed_partner_users` is mediator-specific (seeds two partners from `DISCORD_PARTNER_USER_ID_A/B`) \u2014 keep that behaviour bound to mediator's client only. `catch_up_recent_messages` becomes per-bot. |\n| 12 | Tests | Anything that mocks `discord._token()` or constructs `DiscordGatewayBot()` with the old signature needs to update. Estimated: 6\u201315 test files. |\n\n## 6. Cutover strategy\n\nThe Veas Railway service is **currently live** running mediator on `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN=<mediator's token>` (assumed; verify in prep phase). The deploy must not regress mediator.\n\n### 6.1 Back-compat fallback\n\nDuring the transition, support **both** env var schemes:\n\n1. New: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` (etc.)\n2. Legacy: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` (singular) \u2014 treated as the token for the **single** `bot_id` in the `channels` table that matches, OR ignored if multiple Discord channels exist.\n\nLogic at startup:\n- If any per-bot env var is set, use per-bot lookup.\n- If only `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` is set and exactly one `channels` row has `transport='discord'`, treat it as that bot's token (logs a deprecation warning).\n- If neither matches the registered channels, log a warning and skip those bots.\n\nThis means **prod cutover is a config change, not a code-and-config simultaneous change**:\n\n1. Deploy code with multi-gateway support. Old `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still works for mediator. No regression.\n2. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR=<same value as DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN>` in Railway. Mediator now works under both schemes.\n3. Insert `channels` row for `tante_rosi` (with Rosi's Discord bot user ID as address).\n4. Set `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI=<Rosi's token>` in Railway.\n5. Insert `bots` row + `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`.\n6. Restart. Both gateways come up.\n7. After verification: delete the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var. Confirm mediator still works (it should, via `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR`).\n\nThe fallback can be retired in a follow-up PR once everyone's confident.\n\n### 6.2 What `channels` row currently exists?\n\nThe prep phase **must verify**: query `mediator.channels WHERE transport='discord'` against prod and report what's there. If a mediator `channels` row already exists with the right `address` (mediator's Discord bot user ID), great. If not, we need to insert one as part of the cutover (the legacy code apparently worked without one because of the `_resolve_scope` mediator default).\n\n### 6.3 Test environment\n\nLocal dev / CI typically runs with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` and may have no Discord token at all. The refactor must not break:\n- Startup with no Discord tokens (the gateway-loop section just doesn't run)\n- Startup with `MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` (Discord init is skipped entirely)\n- Tests that mock `discord._token()` or `discord.send_text` (these need updating, but the failure modes should be loud, not silent)\n\n## 7. Acceptance criteria\n\nA reviewer should be able to confirm all of these from the diff + a manual test:\n\n1. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + only legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` set + one `channels` row \u2192 works exactly as before.** Mediator gateway opens, receives messages, replies. No deprecation noise that breaks anything.\n2. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=discord` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` + `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_TANTE_ROSI` + two `channels` rows \u2192 both gateways open.** Each handles only its own DMs. Replies route to the right bot's token (verify with a DM to each).\n3. **Pacer state is per-bot.** Typing to mediator does not affect Rosi's pacer state.\n4. **Adding a third bot is purely config:** insert `bots` row, insert `channels` row, set env var, restart. No code change required.\n5. **All existing tests pass** with adjustments only to the discord-test surface (no broad test-suite shuffling).\n6. **`MESSAGING_PROVIDER=whatsapp` is unchanged.** No Discord init runs; no errors on missing Discord env vars.\n7. **`seed_channels.py` is idempotent under the new scheme** \u2014 running it twice with the same env produces no diff in the table.\n8. **Startup diagnostics list which bot ids are configured for Discord and which are missing tokens.** No token values in logs.\n9. **No regression in pytest collection / type checking.**\n\n## 8. Risks and watchouts\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n|---|---|\n| **Threading `bot_id` through `messaging.py` is touchier than it looks** because outbound messages can be replies to messages that have NULL `bot_id` (legacy mediator rows). | Default to `'mediator'` when the source row's `bot_id` is NULL. Make this explicit in the plan. |\n| **`agentic.py` `add_reaction` call site lacks an obvious `bot_id` source** if the reaction is fire-and-forget. | The agentic turn always has a resolved scope; thread it through. |\n| **Pacer signature change ripples** if we go single-pacer-with-keys instead of per-bot-pacer. | Recommend per-bot pacer; signature unchanged. |\n| **`init_client` / `close_client` module-level lifecycle** is currently keyed off the one global `_client`. With per-bot clients, the dict is the new state. | The lifespan now closes all clients in the dict. Don't forget the `close_client` call in the `finally:` of `lifespan`. |\n| **Cutover env-var ordering** \u2014 if `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN_MEDIATOR` is set but the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` still exists, what wins? | Per-bot env vars always win. Legacy only kicks in when no per-bot match exists for a channel row. Document explicitly. |\n| **Tests that mock the module-level `_token()` or `_client`** will break silently if not updated. | Search for these patterns in prep phase; produce a list of test files needing updates. |\n| **The decode-from-token helper in `discord_id.py`** is called by `seed_channels.py` and by inbound handlers. Both need per-bot variants. | Pass `bot_id` explicitly to the decoder; the env var pattern handles the rest. |\n\n## 9. Deliverables\n\nA single branch, `discord-multi-gateway`, cut from `rosi-pregnancy-bot`. One PR. Passing test suite. The PR description should include:\n\n- Summary of the refactor\n- The new env-var convention\n- Cutover steps for prod (copy-paste of \u00a76.1)\n- A note on what's NOT in the PR (the `bots` / `channels` / `bot_bindings` row inserts for Tante Rosi are a follow-up \u2014 those need the Discord bot ID and the principal user ID, which are operator data)\n\n## 10. Out of scope (don't do these in this PR)\n\n- Inserting prod `bots` row for `tante_rosi`\n- Inserting prod `channels` row for `tante_rosi`\n- Inserting prod `bot_bindings` row for `tante_rosi`\n- Onboarding the principal user to Rosi\n- Changing the WhatsApp transport\n- Retiring the legacy `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` env var (follow-up after cutover)\n- Refactoring the inbound path beyond what's needed to thread `bot_id` through scope resolution (which is already in place)\n\n## 11. References\n\n- `app/services/discord.py:50` \u2014 `_token()` singleton\n- `app/services/discord.py:329-487` \u2014 `DiscordGatewayBot` class\n- `app/services/discord.py:478-481` \u2014 scope resolution that already keys on bot's own address\n- `app/services/routing.py:20` \u2014 `resolve_bot(transport, address)`\n- `app/services/inbound.py:169-209` \u2014 `_resolve_scope`\n- `app/main.py:185-190` \u2014 single-gateway construction\n- `app/main.py:118-145` \u2014 `_build_coalescer` and `DiscordPacer` construction\n- `migrations/0020_topics_bots_bindings.sql` \u2014 `bots`, `channels`, `bot_bindings` schema\n- `scripts/seed_channels.py` \u2014 currently single-token channel seeder\n\n## 12. What the planner should produce\n\nA plan that:\n\n1. Explicitly proposes the `DiscordClient` class shape (constructor, instance methods, where it lives)\n2. Lists the exact signature change for each of the 12 touch points\n3. Resolves the per-bot-pacer vs single-pacer question with a reason\n4. Specifies how each outbound call site sources `bot_id` (the \u00a74.5 question)\n5. Sequences the cutover-back-compat logic correctly (`DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` fallback under what conditions)\n6. Identifies the test files that need updating (prep phase output)\n7. Confirms the `seed_channels.py` idempotency strategy under the new scheme\n8. Has a clear \"this is what 'done' looks like\" against \u00a77\n\nThe plan is allowed to overrule any specific recommendation in this brief if it has a reason \u2014 but it must surface the disagreement explicitly, not silently.",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.
